## Corpus run

`dart run bin/corpus.dart update`, then `check`, on the final tree on top of `c18129b` (cache and work directories in the scratchpad, absolute paths; about 4 minutes on an M-series Mac):

| | `union: raw` (default) | `union: structural` |
|---|---|---|
| Clients | 20, 19 generated (artemis skipped for exactly TOA009, as expected) | 20, 19 generated |
| Files | 13,205 (stripe 6,445, openai 2,109, github 1,807, immich 433, discord 353, jellyfin 346, medusa 338, grace 265, grace_bundle 263, kubernetes 257, airflow 203, ditto 137, googlemaps 104, tambo 99, springdoc 16, petstore 14, swashbuckle 8, efgs 5, digitalocean 3) | 14,554 |
| Diagnostics | 4,000 of 34 codes (TOA107 793, TOA457 755, TOA455 433, TOA651 404, TOA453 360, TOA550 268, TOA658 230, TOA503 165, TOA601 126, ...) | 3,720 of 34 codes |
| `dart analyze --fatal-infos`, `dart format --set-exit-if-changed` | clean | clean |
| Second `generate` | 0 files written or deleted | 0 |
| Examples checked (decoded and round-tripped) | 1,763 | 1,781 |
| Examples that do not match their own schema (spec defects, counted) | 135 (github 124, ditto 3, grace 3, grace_bundle 3, googlemaps 2) | 135 |
| Synthetic values checked | 26,359 | 29,713 |
| Round-trip failures | 0 | 0 |

`corpus.lock`: 40 clients, 27,759 files, 3.7 MB. The replay of this plan wrote a byte-identical lock after each of tasks 3, 4, 5, 7 and 9, and `corpus check` found no difference after the refactors of tasks 6 and 8. How the numbers moved: task 5 adds 1,654 synthetic values (OpenAI's variants selected by their constants: 807 `raw`, 847 `structural`), TOA457 x755 and TOA456 x4; task 7 decodes one more GitHub example (a directory listing the lock had counted as a spec defect) and removes two failures; task 9 removes the last one.

## Fuzz

`dart run bin/fuzz.dart` on the final tree: 200 seeds, 1,200 builds (six clients per seed), 10,750 files, 17 sampled outputs analyzed and their literals evaluated, 0 problems, about 10 seconds; 2,000 seeds, 12,000 builds, 107,452 files, 167 sampled, 0 problems, about 55 seconds. Every build was compared with a second build (no difference), every file and every text report checked for leaks (none), every sampled output passed `dart analyze --fatal-infos`, and every spec string of its IR was among its evaluated literals. The runs found three problems of the checks themselves, fixed in the tool before the plan's blocks were taken: a hostile name `*/ injected` became the enum constant `injected` at the start of a line (the hostile names no longer contain the leak sentinel); a tab in an `operationId` stays raw in a doc span by design (`DartCode` drops controls but keeps the tab); and since task 7 a string variant of a discriminator union is picked by its JSON type, so its discriminator value is no literal (`SpecStrings` keeps only those of object variants). No seed found a generator defect, so no fixture was added.

## Defects found

| Defect | Found by | In this plan |
|---|---|---|
| A union variant whose type is a nullable list wrote `value == null ? null : [for (... in value) ...]`, which does not compile (GitHub `pull-request-stacks` 422 errors) | the analyzer on the corpus | Fixed: task 1, fixture `nullable_variants.yaml` |
| A discriminator without a `mapping` took the schema name as the value, while OpenAI's variants fix the property to a `const`: no JSON selected them, real responses decoded as unknown | round trip (2,058 synthetic values, all OpenAI) | Decided by the user and done: task 5 (TOA457), tests in `discriminator_derived_test.dart` |
| A union with a discriminator whose variant is an array (GitHub `repos/get-content`: a directory is a JSON array) read the discriminator from an object, so a real directory listing did not decode | round trip (synthetic, and a GitHub example) | Fixed: tasks 6 (refactor) and 7, fixture `discriminator_array.yaml` |
| An optional constant was always written by `toJson`, so in a `structural` `oneOf` whose branches overlap the value matched several branches after the round trip and came back as unknown (Medusa `SearchOptionsFacetsItem`) | round trip (synthetic) | Fixed: tasks 8 (refactor) and 9, fixture `optional_constant.yaml` |
| SARIF reports lacked what GitHub code scanning requires (rule descriptions and help, partial fingerprints, regions), and a result without a file (TOA901) had no location, which makes GitHub reject the upload | the offline check of task 14 | Fixed: task 14 |

## Deferred

Written into the roadmap's "Moved from M11" (task 16): `style: cookie` (a user decision); the CI-only cells (SDK 3.10.0, macOS, Windows, the nightly jobs), which the first CI run verifies. Out of scope by the spec: real uploads to code scanning, `diff` between regenerations (M12).

## Self-review

Spec decisions: 1 - task 2 (`corpus.yaml` with URL, SHA-256, license, expectation; `fetch` with a cache and hash verification; a mismatch is an error; the regular tests stay offline). 2 - tasks 3-5, 7, 9 (`corpus.lock` with the hash of every file and of the output, diagnostics by code; `check` lists files and codes; `update` rewrites it in the commit of the change; the goldens of the small fixtures stay). 3 - task 4 (examples of schemas and responses, synthetic values for required fields, every union variant and enum boundaries; `fromJson` -> `toJson` -> `fromJson`; spec defects counted in the lock, not failed), task 5 (OpenAI's variants checked too), tasks 7 and 9 (every value comes back equal). 4 - task 12 (every PR: ubuntu x 3.10.0/stable, Chrome dart2js and dart2wasm, macOS and Windows; the corpus job with both SDKs and the four checks; nightly beta as a warning and the corpus on Windows). 5 - task 10 (`tool/fuzz` as `bin/fuzz.dart`, 200 seeds per PR, 6000 nightly; hostile strings at names, descriptions, enum values, property keys, media types and event names, and every form of status key (codes, ranges, `default`); the three checks; no generator defect, so no fixture). 6 - task 2 (the five, the 14, three code-first; artemis with `check-errors`; the private spec never used). 7 - task 11. 8 - tasks 12-15 (Windows drive letters, SARIF against the schema and GitHub's rules, the browser's `Cookie`, `1.0` and NaN on dart2js, case and hard links on Linux and Windows), and `style: cookie` deferred as a design decision. Coordinator additions A-E: tasks 5, 6-7, 8-9, 14, 15. Gates: every package's suite, analyze and format after every task; the corpus end to end with absolute scratch paths; the decoding tests on the whole corpus; the fuzz at 200 and 2000 seeds; each defect fixed in its own task with a regression fixture. No placeholders; names and signatures between tasks were checked by the replay from `main`.

## Open questions

Each is a choice the spec leaves open; the plan implements the default given.

1. **The corpus runs both `union` modes** (40 clients in the lock, 3.7 MB). Alternative: `raw` only, half the time and size, but the `structural` code would lose its analyzer gate on real specs.
2. **The lock keeps full SHA-256 hashes per file** (the spec's wording). Alternative: 16 hex characters, a smaller lock with the same practical safety.
3. **A round-trip failure is counted in the lock, not fatal by itself** (0 today), so a new or a fixed failure fails `check` through the lock. Alternative: fail `check` on any round-trip failure.
4. **The FastAPI code-first spec is airflow**, already in the corpus (FastAPI's own template does not commit its `openapi.json`); the new code-first specs are Immich (NestJS), springdoc's Spring Data REST test result and Swashbuckle's .NET 10 test site. Alternative: look for a larger FastAPI project that commits its spec.
5. **`semver` judges the lock only once the base has one**, so the M11 pull request itself (which adds the lock and changes output in tasks 1, 5, 7 and 9) is judged by the goldens alone; the CHANGELOG records every change under "Output changes" and "Fixed", and the version stays 0.0.1 (unreleased). Alternative: raise it to 0.1.0 in M11.
6. **Format and analysis run on stable only**; SDK 3.10.0 runs the tests and the corpus. Alternative: also analyze with 3.10.0, which may reject lints newer than it.
7. **The Windows and macOS jobs run every suite on each PR** as the spec says, but this plan could run neither; a failure there on the first run is fixed in a follow-up task (the tests that need `ln`, `kill` or POSIX modes already skip on Windows).
8. **TOA457 is reported only when the derived value differs from the component name**, once per variant (755 infos in the `raw` corpus, all but 2 OpenAI). Alternative: report every derived value, or one summary per union.
9. **An optional constant of a model made in code is written** (M8 decision 8); only a decoded model remembers the absent ones. Alternative: never write optional constants (simpler models, but a request would lose a constant a server dispatches on, such as OpenAI's `type: message`).
10. **The vendored SARIF schema carries a `NOTICE.md` that names the OASIS copyright, the IPR policy and the standard's "Notices" section, and paraphrases its terms**; it does not reproduce the notice word for word. Before merging, paste the exact OASIS copyright notice from the standard's "Notices" section into `NOTICE.md` if a verbatim copy is wanted. Alternative: have `corpus fetch` download the schema and skip the test without it (the tests would no longer be offline-complete).
11. **`style: cookie` (OpenAPI 3.2)** stays percent-encoded like `form`; sending RFC 6265 cookie-octets unencoded is a user decision, parked.
12. **A SARIF result without a file is located at the config file** when `check` writes it; a programmatic `SarifReporter()` without `defaultFile` leaves such a result without a location (GitHub would reject it). Alternative: make `defaultFile` required.
